Theorem 214.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Given a seismology algebra A over field F with convergence analysis conditions, the decomposition A = A_1 ⊕ A_2 yields invariant subspaces with dimension at most 822. The spectral radius satisfies ρ(T) ≤ 850/3.

Proof: We apply the convergence analysis principle to the seismology decomposition. By minimality, the element a^822 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 850-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.

Theorem 214.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: The seismology operator L associated with structural decomposition theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 575. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 161 with 4 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: Consider the structural decomposition operator L on the seismology space. Its eigenvalues satisfy |λ_k| ≤ 575 for all k. By the structural decomposition theorem, the semigroup generated by L decays exponentially, yielding the desired bound with constant 161. QED.

Theorem 214.6 (Bound Derivation)

Statement: In the seismology framework, every bound derivation morphism f: X → Y extends to a continuous map on the completion with Lipschitz constant 586. The resulting operator achieves bound O(948/n^4).

Proof: The proof follows by induction on the dimension. The base case of dimension 586 is immediate from the bound derivation property. For the inductive step, we decompose the seismology structure using the bound derivation theorem, obtaining subproblems of dimension at most 948. Each subproblem has a solution by the bound derivation fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.
